### 222 — admin API · POST /send-otp type 2 (verify) answered "Trying to get property 'otp' of non-object" when no OTP exists for today
- Files: `maidmatch-admin-api-nodejs/src/misc/misc.service.ts` (sendVerificationOtp), `src/misc/misc.spec.ts`, `src/core/i18n/resources/{en,in,zh_HK,zh_CN}.lang.json` (+`OTP_NOT_SENT`).
- REQ-ID: user 13 Oct 16:40 IST — Postman `POST {{admin_backend_node}}/send-otp` (type 2) → 200 `{status:false, message:"Trying to get property 'otp' of non-object"}`; Nest log `MiscService.sendVerificationOtp (misc.service.ts:254)`.
- Cause: the Laravel source read `(string)$otps->otp` without checking that an `otps_log` row exists for `send_to` + `verification_type` **today** (`DATE(created_at) = CURDATE()`), so a verify before any send (or on the next day, or with a `send_to` that differs from the one the OTP was sent to — e.g. WhatsApp stores country code + number) died with that PHP error. The Node port reproduced it on purpose (`throw MiscService.nullProperty('otp')`).
- Change: that case now returns the normal failure envelope `{status:false, message: OTP_NOT_SENT}` — "No OTP was sent to this number or email today. Please request a new OTP." (4 languages). Matching / mismatching OTP paths unchanged. Spec updated (no-row → OTP_NOT_SENT).
- Checks: admin `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors. Note for testing: type 1 and type 2 must use the same `send_to` **and** `verification_type`; for `whatsapp_verified` the stored value is `send_to_country_code + send_to`, so verify with the same two fields; the row must be from today.
- Status: Done — restart the admin API.

### 228 — Employer Dashboard · server-side Mobile / WhatsApp masking + exact-number search — backend (admin API), per domain/DASH.md §6–§7
- Files: admin API `src/employer/contact-mask.ts` (new: `maskNumber`, `maskContactRow`, `parseNumberQuery`, `exactNumberPredicate`), `src/employer/contact-mask.spec.ts` (new, 6 tests), `src/employer/employer.service.ts` (`getEmployerList`: every row passes `maskContactRow` before the Restricted cap / pagination), `src/employer/employer.repository.ts` (`employerList` search: `u.mobile_number LIKE ?` removed; equality predicate on Mobile and WhatsApp when the query is a complete number).
- REQ-ID: user 14 Oct 13:58 IST — "admin list mai mobile number masking ka feature hai na, woh implement kar sakte ho? pahle md files or html dekhlo". Sources read: `domain/DASH.md` §5–§7, `CURRENT_REQUIREMENTS.md` → Employers ("Mobile/WhatsApp masking on the Employer listing applies consistently; full-value search must not leak unmasked values"), `WORKFLOWS.json` Employer Dashboard walkthrough ("Mobile and WhatsApp are masked for every viewer … 91824467 finds exactly one row, still masked … 4467 finds nothing"), prototype `implementation/source/modules/employer-dashboard/parts/listing.js` (`UNIVERSAL_MASK = ['mobile','whatsapp']`, `maskNumber`, `sameNumber`, `exactMatch`). Our frontend `Views/Pages/Employer/newui/employerDashboardColumns.js` already states "Mobile and WhatsApp arrive already masked from the server" — this entry is the server half.
- Reading taken: masking is UNIVERSAL (every populated Mobile / WhatsApp on every listing row, every viewer). DASH.md §6.2 still describes the older "selective, fixture metadata" variant; the written requirement ("applies consistently"), the workflow ("for every viewer") and the current prototype source all say universal, and universal is also the stricter reading. No designation column, no selection UI (§6.2 explicitly defers one).
- Behaviour (`POST /get-employer-list`, all consumers — dashboard, dedupe search, invoice employer pickers): `mobile_number` / `whatsapp_number` → `•••• 1234` (U+2022 per hidden digit, last four kept, one space); `country_code` / `whatsapp_country_code` unchanged in their own fields; null / '' untouched; ≤ 4 digits returned as digits. No admin bypass, no new permission, no change to record access (§6.1). `get-employer-detail` untouched (§6.5). Search: Mobile is out of the LIKE index (WhatsApp never was in it); a query made only of digits / spaces / `+` / `-` / `.` / `()` with ≥ 6 digits also matches by EQUALITY on either number — rule 1 `digits(cc + number) = digits(query)`, rule 2 `digits(number) = national(query) AND (query has no cc OR stored cc empty OR cc equal)` — via `REGEXP_REPLACE(...,'[^0-9]','')` (MariaDB ≥ 10.0.5 / MySQL 8; staging is MariaDB — the list query already relies on JSON_OBJECT). A partial number matches nothing. Existing `employer_access_type` Restricted cap and `employer_global_list` gate unchanged (§7.1).
- Checks: admin `tsc` (service + repository + graph) — 0 errors; the four helpers executed in the sandbox against 21 assertions (mask format, null handling, row masking, query parsing, predicate args) — 21/21; the generated predicate executed on MariaDB 10.11 with a 6-row fixture for 10 queries — every result as the spec's examples (`91824467` → rows with that national number in any cc; `+852 9182 4467` → the 852 row only; `(852) 9450-6673` → rule 1; `+65 6123 4587` → the row whose stored cc is empty; `5511 8264` → via WhatsApp; `4467` / `9182` → not a number search). CRLF preserved.
- Frontend notes (no backend action): (1) `formatPhone` renders `(852) •••• 1234`; the spec format is `+852 •••• 1234` — cosmetic, their call. (2) §7.3: never echo the complete query outside the input (results count, empty state). (3) Invoice `EmployerResultTable` and `DedupeSearch` now show masked numbers too — dedupe still works by typing the complete number.
- DB / env: none. Deploy: restart admin API.

### 229 — Contracts dashboard · fix: the row's processing group was read from the category CSV (NaN → Group 1 rail) — backend (contract API)
- Files: contract API `src/services/contract.dashboard.services.ts` (`processingGroupSql()`; used for `group_no` in the select and for the `group` filter).
- REQ-ID: user 14 Oct 15:39 IST screenshot of Contract Details LFTR000666 (Filipino · Local Terminated (TR)) showing the Group 1 rail (Contract · Consulate · Immigration · Visa · Insurance · Deployment) while PROCESS.md §6 table puts Filipino + TR in **Group 4** (Contract → Insurance → HK Medical → Immigration → Visa → Consulate). Found while answering "insurance tab ka sab okay hai?".
- Cause: `contract_categories.processing_group` is a VARCHAR CSV of the groups a category can belong to ("3,4" for TR; `contract.category.repository.getListByProcessingGroup` reads it with FIND_IN_SET). The dashboard selected it as `group_no` and did `Number(r.group_no)` → NaN → `group: null`, `stage_order: STAGE_ORDER[1]`; the details page (which takes `row.stage_order` from this list) therefore drew Group 1's rail for every multi-group category. The `group` filter (`cc.processing_group = ?`) had the same defect (never matched a CSV value).
- Fix: the group is now computed per row in SQL with the same rule as `getProcessingGroup()` (worker nationality × category × wait-in-Macau, env ids): 1 = IND/THA/BGD + LT (not wait-in-Macau) / Overseas / Direct hire; 2 = Filipino + the same; 3 = IND/THA/BGD + Local Finished / Local Transfer / TR / Renew / LT wait-in-Macau; 4 = Filipino + the same; NULL otherwise (falls back to Group 1's order as before). The same expression drives the `group` filter. `contract_categories.processing_group` stays what it is (the category option lists still use it — #12).
- Checks: contract `tsc` (dashboard + details graph) — 0 errors; the expression executed on MariaDB with 9 nationality × category × wait-in-Macau combinations — 9/9 as the §6 table (Filipino TR → 4, Indo TR → 3, Filipino LT → 2, Filipino LT wait-in-Macau → 4, Thai overseas → 1, Filipino renew → 4, BGD direct hire → 1, unknown nationality / category → NULL). CRLF preserved.
- Note for the test record: LFTR000666's Consulate "Returned" and Deployment "Completed" are leftover data from the old flow; with the Group 4 rail they are simply not shown (Group 4 has no Deployment stage).
- DB / env: none (uses the existing CONTRACT_CATEGORY_* / *_NATIONALITY_ID envs). Deploy: restart contract API.

### 230 — Worker Profile Status hierarchy · getWorkerStatus re-ordered (Employed / Hired before the profile checks, sticky statuses never overridden) — backend (admin API, mobile API)
- Files: admin API `src/worker/worker-status.engine.ts` (new order; `STICKY_STATUSES`; `WorkerStatusOptions.respectSticky`), `src/worker/worker.repository.ts` (`contractByWorker` prefers the Active contract), `src/worker/worker.service.ts` (`resolvePreviousStatus` passes `respectSticky: false`); mobile API `src/shared/general-helper.service.ts` (`getWorkerStatus` same order; `C.WS_SUSPENDED/BANNED/HOLD/PARTNER_PENDING/PARTNER_WITHDRAW` added; `contractByWorkerId` prefers Active).
- REQ-ID: user 14 Oct 22:16 IST — the Profile Status hierarchy table (1 Banned · 2 Suspended · 3 Hold · 4 Partner withdrawn · 5 Not Registered (Partner Pending) · 6 Employed = Contract Active · 7 Hired = Contract Processing · 8 Holiday Mode · 9 Not Visible · 10 Incomplete · 11 In review · 12 Inactive · 13 Available) + "employed workers ka checking pahle hoga" + 23:05 "mobile api or admin api mai ye base function hai or unmai jitna function hai us hisabse ordering fix kardo".
- Before: both engines went Incomplete → In review → Holiday → verified gate (→ Available, and only then Hired / Employed from the contract) → Not Visible; Banned / Suspended / Hold / Partner statuses were not looked at, so every caller that writes the engine's answer back (worker save / validate / write, app profile save, auto-status cron) could flip a Hold / Suspended / Banned worker to Incomplete or Available; a worker with an incomplete profile and an Active contract was Incomplete, not Employed; admin's Holiday test used the OPPOSITE location rule to mobile's (in HK/Macau vs outside).
- After (both engines, identical order): (1–5) current status ∈ {Banned, Suspended, Hold, Partner withdrawn, Partner pending} → answered as it is (callers see "no change"); (6) contract Active and not "looking for a new employer" → Employed; (7) contract Processing → Hired (when a worker has both, the Active one decides — `ORDER BY status = Active DESC`); (8) Local + current_location outside HK / Macau + holiday flag → Holiday (the table's wording; admin now matches mobile); (9) is_private → Not Visible; (10) the unchanged Incomplete predicate; (11) the unchanged In review predicate; (12) Inactive stays the scheduler's job (not decided here); (13) the unchanged verified gate → Available. The verified gate no longer carries the contract branch. An Active contract whose worker is "looking for a new employer" is still not Employed — it falls through to the profile checks (Available when verified), as before. "Back to previous" (admin status change from Hold / Suspended / Banned / Not Visible / Partner withdrawn back to a derived status) calls the engine with `respectSticky: false` so it derives from the profile instead of echoing the sticky value.
- Checks: admin `tsc` (engine + service + repository graph) — 0 errors; mobile `tsc` (general-helper graph) — 0 errors in the touched file (pre-existing `ctx.ts requestId` augmentation noise only); the admin engine executed in the sandbox with a stub repository against 17 scenarios (sticky stays even when incomplete; back-to-previous derives; Employed before Incomplete / Holiday; Hired before In review; Active + looking falls through; Holiday only outside HK; Holiday before Not Visible; Not Visible before Incomplete; Incomplete before In review; Available; no mobile → 0) — 17/17. CRLF preserved.
- Not changed: the Incomplete / In review / verified predicates themselves (1:1 ports); the no-show → Suspended / Banned rule, partner withdraw, send-link (they set the sticky statuses); the scheduler inactivity job (60 / 90 days). Product note: with Employed / Hired ahead of the profile checks, an Employed worker's incomplete profile is no longer visible through the status — the Profile section statuses still show it on the record.
- DB / env: none. Deploy: restart admin API + mobile API. The auto-status cron (`updateAutomaticStatus`) and the next profile save will move existing workers to the new order's answer (previous_status + log row kept per change).

### 231 — Worker Profile Status hierarchy (part 2) · the direct status writers respect the order too — backend (admin API, mobile API, scheduler)
- Files: admin API `src/worker/worker.repository.ts` (`updateWorker` guard + `statusIsProtected`; `contractByWorker` already prefers Active), `src/worker/worker.service.ts` (`changeStatus` writes with `{ force: true }`); mobile API `src/worker/worker-repo.helper.ts` (same guard on `WorkerRepo.updateWorker`), `src/worker/worker-profile.service.ts` (Holiday Mode ON derives the status through `getWorkerStatus` instead of its own "frozen" list; the expired-visa / expired-contract Incomplete write skips workers whose status ranks above Incomplete — `ABOVE_INCOMPLETE`); scheduler `src/repository/worker.repository.ts` (`getWorkersAfterVisaOrContractEndDateExpired` excludes the sticky statuses).
- REQ-ID: continuation of 230 (user 15 Oct 10:53 "continue"). 230 re-ordered the engine, but ~15 flows write `status` directly (admin profile save: expired visa / contract, missing experience → Incomplete; document verify → Incomplete / In review; contract status changes; private toggle; mobile complete-profile; mobile Holiday Mode; the scheduler's 14-day expiry job) and would still have replaced a Hold / Suspended / Banned / Employed / Hired / Holiday worker with Incomplete or In review.
- Rule (both repositories' single write choke point): a derived status — Incomplete (1), In review (2), Available (3), Not Visible (8) — is never written while the current status ranks above it and its condition still holds: Banned / Suspended / Hold / Partner withdrawn / Partner pending (always); Employed (an Active contract exists and the worker is not "looking for a new employer" — the saved `job_status` counts); Hired (a Processing contract exists); Holiday Mode (holiday flag still 1 after the save, Local worker, current location outside HK / Macau). The other saved fields go through; only `status` / `previous_status` are dropped. Admin **Change Status** (incl. "back to previous") passes `force: true` — it is the explicit act that ends a sticky status. The engine's own answers never conflict with the guard (it returns the sticky / Employed / Hired / Holiday value under exactly those conditions). Mobile Holiday Mode ON now: save flag + location, then `status = getWorkerStatus()` — sticky and Employed / Hired stay, Incomplete / In review / Not Visible / Available become Holiday (the old list also froze Incomplete / In review, which the hierarchy puts below Holiday); Holiday OFF already re-derived.
- Checks: admin `tsc` (repository + service + engine graph) — 0 errors; mobile `tsc` (worker-repo.helper + worker-profile.service graph) — 0 errors in the touched files; scheduler `tsc` — 0. CRLF preserved.
- Not changed: the interview no-show rule (2nd → Suspended, 3rd → Banned — already in both APIs), partner withdraw / send-link (sticky setters), the inactivity job (Available ↔ Inactive only), `updateAutomaticStatus` (engine-driven).
- DB / env: none. Deploy: restart admin API, mobile API, scheduler.

Any → 409 `REVERT_BLOCKED_POST_HIRE` with `data.post_hire_actions[] {code, text, ref, at}` and `can_override` (admin / sub-admin only). Admin resends with `override_reason` (mandatory → 409 `REVERT_BLOCKED_POST_HIRE_OVERRIDE_REASON_REQUIRED` when blank); non-admin cannot override.
- Side effects: worker profile status re-derived (existing engine) and the previously commented "up" shift re-enabled for Active → Processing only (previous work experience back to Current Contract when the current one is blank; Cancelled → Processing never shifts). ONE Activity event (`contract_status_update`): "Contract X status updated from Active to Processing — <reason> · administrator override: <override> · reopened: Visa, Deployment · Contract Start and Expected Contract End kept; Entry Visa Expiry and Visa Expiry retained as recorded; back on the Processing dashboard"; `json_data.reversion` carries reason, override, post-hire list, stages and per-stage from/to. ### 243 — Employer · the 7 frontend asks of 16 Oct (E-1…E-7 / U-0032, U-0039, U-0057) (16 Oct 2026)

Source: `employer_BACKEND_ASKS_2026-10-16.md` + the PM rulings in the Conflicts sheet (U-0029, U-0031, U-0032, U-0033, U-0039, U-0042, U-0046, U-0051, U-0055, U-0057). Answers: `employer_BACKEND_ANSWERS_2026-10-16.md`. SQL: `employer_E_batch_2026-10-16.sql` (plain SQL, run once; tested on a schema copy). Admin API only.

**E-1 / U-0032 — duplicate phone number warns, admin confirms**
- `employer/employer.repository.ts` — new `phoneDuplicates(db, probes, userId)` (+ `PhoneDuplicate` type): same match set as `whatsappDuplicate` (users.mobile_number resolved to the employer / worker / staff / user behind the login; employers / workers whatsapp; staffs whatsapp / personal_mobile; country-code aware; own user excluded) but returns the matched rows (source, id, reference_id, name, country_code, number).
- `employer/employer.service.ts` — `saveEmployer`: the two hard refusals (`WHATSAPP_NUMBER_DUPLICATE`, "The mobile number has already been taken.") replaced by `phoneDuplicateWarning()`: without `confirm_duplicate` → 200 `status:false, code: "DUPLICATE_MOBILE", data { fields[], confirm_field, duplicates[] }` with numbers masked (`maskNumber`), nothing written; with `confirm_duplicate: true | 1 | "true" | "1"` the save proceeds. `EmployerService.duplicateConfirmed()` static. Email keeps `unique:users,email` (U-0033 pending).
- `misc/misc.service.ts` — `update-contact-details` `type: employer`: a changed WhatsApp number now warns the same way (own user excluded) instead of `WHATSAPP_NUMBER_DUPLICATE`; workers keep the refusal.
- `employer/employer.controller.ts` — `save-employer` accepts `confirm_duplicate`.
- Noted for the PM: `users.mobile_number` has no unique index; the employer app's OTP login resolves a duplicate Mobile with `LIMIT 1` (older account wins). Built as ruled; one-line revert if Mike wants Mobile to stay a block.

**E-3 / U-0057 — employer locations Hong Kong + Macau only**
- `reference/employer-locations.ts` (new) — `HONG_KONG_COUNTRY_ID = 1`, `MACAU_COUNTRY_ID = 119`, `EMPLOYER_LOCATION_COUNTRY_IDS`, `isEmployerLocationCountry`.
- `reference/reference.repository.ts` — `country('employer')`: `AND id IN (1, 119) ORDER BY FIELD(id, 1, 119)`. `reference/reference.service.ts` — `country()` filters + orders the rows **after** the Redis cache read (24 h master TTL) so a list cached before the fix cannot show Taiwan.
- SQL §2 — `countries.employer = 0` for every row other than 1 / 119, `= 1` for 1 / 119; pre / post check.

**E-6 / U-0039 — spoken Primary Language gets its own storage**
- Finding: `spoken_language` = `users.language_id` = the employer app's UI locale; `maidmatch-mobileapi-nodejs` `auth/user-auth.guard.ts` rewrites it to the app's `language` header (en / zh_HK / zh_CN) on every authenticated request and `common.service.ts` `updateLanguageService` sets it from the app. A spoken value there would not survive the next login; adding Cantonese / Mandarin rows to `languages` would create app locales. Hence a new column.
- `employer/primary-language.ts` (new) — `PRIMARY_LANGUAGES = [cantonese, english, mandarin]`, `PRIMARY_LANGUAGE_RULE`, `primaryLanguageFromRequest` (not sent / empty = leave alone), `primaryLanguageFields(stored, legacyName, t)` → `primary_language`, `primary_language_label`, `primary_language_legacy` (the users.language name as stored while no spoken value exists — never converted), `primaryLanguageOptions`.
- `employer/employer.repository.ts` — list selects `e.primary_language` + the four `languages.name_*` as `legacy_language_name_*`; new filter `primaryLanguage` (`e.primary_language IN (…)`; legacy values match only All).
- `employer/employer.service.ts` — list rows and `get-employer-detail` carry the three fields (legacy name locale-resolved via `resolveName`); `addEmployerData` (save-employer create / update) and `updateEmployerBasicDetails` write `primary_language` only when sent. `spoken_language` / `spoken_language_info` and the legacy `users.language_id` write stay for the old UI.
- `employer/employer.controller.ts` — `primary_language: nullable|in:cantonese,english,mandarin` on `save-employer` and `update-employer-basic` (there `spoken_language` is required only when no `primary_language` is sent); list filter `primary_language[]`.
- `reference/reference.controller.ts` + `reference.service.ts` — new `GET get-primary-language` (static, ordered, localized; `id` = `code` = value).
- Lang json en / zh_HK / zh_CN / in — `PHONE_NUMBER_DUPLICATE_WARNING`, `PRIMARY_LANGUAGE_CANTONESE / _ENGLISH / _MANDARIN`.
- SQL §1 — `employers.primary_language VARCHAR(16) NULL` + index. No data migration (U-0039: no conversion).

**Confirmed, no code:** E-2 / U-0029 (`get-employer-list` masks every row for every viewer since the masking entry; the dedupe search uses the same list), E-7 / U-0055 (Invoice has no Details View key — tab gated on `invoice_access` / `invoice_access_cs_non_owner`; Contract `processing_cs_(non_)owner_details`; Insurance `insurance_details_view`).
**❓ product first:** E-4 / U-0046 (Ban permission + reason / category), E-5 / U-0051 (country code on Contact / Home Telephone), U-0031 / U-0042 (masking in the dedupe dialog / Invoice & Insurance pickers — the pickers are in the contract and insurance APIs).

### 251 — Job Offer · `get-eligible-cs-staff` failed on staging with MySQL error 3065 (DISTINCT + ORDER BY) — admin API (19 Oct 2026)

Source: staging report 19 Oct — `POST /get-eligible-cs-staff { job_id: 347 }` → `Raw query failed. Code 3065 … ORDER BY clause is not in SELECT list, references column staffs.first_name … incompatible with DISTINCT`.

- `jobs/jobs.repository.ts` `eligibleCsStaff` — dropped the `DISTINCT`: `staffs.id` is the key and the `users` / `roles` joins are to primary keys, so the rows were unique anyway; the `ORDER BY staffs.first_name, staffs.last_name` stays. MySQL 8 under `ONLY_FULL_GROUP_BY` rejects `DISTINCT` with an ORDER BY on a non-selected column; the MariaDB copy used for the 232 sandbox let it through, which is why it was not caught.
- Scanned every other `SELECT DISTINCT` in the admin API (reference cache, misc search-staff, worker-list owner pools, worker initials) and the other Node repos: the remaining ones either have no ORDER BY or order by a selected alias — fine on MySQL 8.

Tests: narrowed tsc clean. No SQL, no env; restart admin API.

### 252 — Processing checklists · Prepared / Ready marks are the assigned register owner's act (parent record brought in line with Extend Visa §5.3) — admin API (21 Oct 2026)

Source: Priyanka's question on Contract 27 ("jo banda Prepared owner hai wohi Prepared mark kar sakta hai?"); EXTEND.md §5.3 (citing the Processing Details prototype `b22RoleState` and CPERM §8: "A mark is made by the assigned register owner, not by anyone holding Stage Actions — Edit"; Unassigned → that register's marks read-only), OWNERS.md maker / checker, CURRENT_REQUIREMENTS 185 ("sign-off … distinct"). Until now only the Extend Visa case endpoint (`/extend-visa/mark`, processing API, S2) enforced this; the parent Contract / Consulate / Immigration checklist saves were gated by Stage Actions — Edit alone, and S4 (223) had left the ticks untouched. Decision: build (option 1) — the rule is written in the spec; no product question raised.

**admin API**
- `processing/sign-off-authority.ts` (new) — `registerOf`, `changedMarks(request, doc, fields)` (only marks whose value actually changes against the stored document — the screens save the whole document), `signOffRefusal(contract, actorStaffId, changed)` (Prepared judged first; `unassigned` / `not_holder`), `signOffAuthority(contract, actorStaffId, canEdit)` → `{ can_mark_prepared, can_mark_ready, prepared_blocker, ready_blocker, *_owner_staff_id }` with blockers `no_edit` / `unassigned` / `not_holder`.
- `processing/processing.repository.ts` — `contractDetailsById` selects `contracts.prepared_owner_staff_id`, `contracts.ready_owner_staff_id` (S4 columns).
- `processing/processing.service.ts` — `updateFullDetails(ctx, request, fields, actorStaffId)` refuses a changed mark the actor may not make: 403 `code` `NOT_PREPARED_OWNER` · `NOT_READY_OWNER` · `PREPARED_OWNER_UNASSIGNED` · `READY_OWNER_UNASSIGNED`, `data { register, holder_staff_id, fields }`; the consequential Ready clear on a Prepared clear (applySignOffRules) stays the Prepared owner's act. `details(…, actor)` adds `sign_off` to the data.
- `processing/processing.controller.ts` — `stageActionsGate` returns `{ contract, staff, key }`; `update-processing-full-details` passes the staff id; `get-processing-details` computes `canEdit` (same key, mode edit) and passes the actor. No admin bypass — exactly as `/extend-visa/mark` (an admin with no staff row is `not_holder`).
- Lang json ×4 — the four keys.

**Not changed:** `update-processing-documents` (only `_uploaded` fields), Uploaded / Reviewed ticks, Immigration Submit / Return, Owners popover / Manage owners (S4), Extend Visa case page (already compliant). Frontend work order: `frontend-prompt-processing-signoff-252_2026-10-21.md`.

Tests: narrowed tsc over the four files clean; `tsx` sandbox 10 checks (no-change resend judged nothing; changed set; holder ok; maker ≠ checker; Ready owner cannot touch Prepared; Unassigned; no staff row; authority flags ×3). No SQL, no env; restart admin API.

### 259 — Reports (HRMS) · the five report counts failed on MySQL 8 (primary key prepended beside COUNT) — hrms API (22 Oct 2026)

Source: staging 22 Oct — `POST /hrms/reports/insurance-list` → `total_count: { error: "… expression #1 of SELECT list contains nonaggregated column 'contractInsuranceModel.id' … only_full_group_by" }`, empty report. Cause: the count calls `convertToObject(...)` with `raw` left at its default `false`; a non-raw Sequelize `findAll` with includes prepends the model's primary key to the SELECT (it needs it to build instances), so the query became `SELECT id, COUNT(DISTINCT id) …` — fine on MariaDB, refused by MySQL 8. The includes already carried `attributes: []`, so this was the only extra column.

- `src/services/leave.apply.services.ts` — `total_leave_history_count` (leave history list) gets the same `raw: true` (the two leave-apply counts already had it; the time-request counts have no includes, so no primary key is added there).
- `src/services/reports.services.ts` — the five count queries (`total_leave_apply_count`, `total_session_history_count`, `total_job_count`, `total_insurance_count`, `total_contract_count`) now pass `raw: true` to `convertToObject` (`null, null, null, null, null, true`); `getCountAllList` reads `result[0].count` as before. List queries unchanged.

Tests: narrowed tsc — no error in `reports.services.ts` (the 12 pre-existing `enum` typing errors in `src/models/*` are from the Sequelize typings drift, not this change). No SQL, no env; restart hrms API.

### 263 — User Permissions · save with version (409 STALE_VERSION), one audit event per changed value, change-history endpoint (P-4) — settings API (23 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend `user_permissions_BACKEND_ASKS_2026-10-22.md` P-4; 22 Oct package "Review and saving" (recheck version, commit all or none, no-change Save = no event), AT-032 (stale version → reviewable comparison), AT-035 (exactly one event per actual change). No product decision involved.

- SQL `db_queries_2026-10-15/14_permission_version_audit_2026-10-23.sql` (run once, guarded): `staffs.permission_version INT UNSIGNED NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`; table `permission_change_logs (staff_id, user_id, key_value, old_value, new_value, actor_user_id, source ENUM save|copy|schema_migration|eligibility, version, created_at)` — append-only, no update / delete route.
- `GET /permission/list?staff_id` → `{ data, version }` (`version` = the staff's current `permission_version`; `list-without-staff` unchanged).
- `POST /permission/save { staff_id, data, version?, source? }` — one transaction: staff row locked `FOR UPDATE`; `version` present and ≠ current → **409** `{ status:false, code:"STALE_VERSION", message, current_version, changed_since:[{ key_value, old_value, new_value, version, source, created_at, actor_user_id, actor_name }] }`, nothing written; otherwise every key is compared with its stored value and only changed ones are written; `changes.length > 0` → one log row per changed key with `version = current + 1`, `staffs.permission_version = current + 1`; no change → nothing written, version unchanged, no event. Success → `{ status, message, data: [written rows], version, changed }`. `source` (`save` default, `copy`) is recorded on every event. Cache invalidation + `perm.user_changed` bus publish run after commit, once per changed key / once per save. `version` absent → no concurrency check (frontend sends it from 334–338 on).
- `GET /permission/history?staff_id&page&per_page` (max 200) → `{ status:true, data:[events newest first with actor_name], total, page, per_page, version }`. Gate: `checkPermission("view_user")` (+ `users_access`) until `view_permission_history` exists (P-3 batch) — `view_user` / `view_permission_history` added to the Users-module mapping in `check.global.permission.utils.js` (`view_user` was not mapped, so it denied every non-admin).
- Files: `models/permission.change.log.model.js` (new), `models/index.js`, `models/staffs.model.js` (`permission_version`), `repository/permission.change.log.repository.js` (new: `createMany`, `changedSince`, `listByStaff`), `repository/index.js`, `repository/permission.repository.js` (create / update / read take `{ transaction }`, read locks inside one), `repository/staffs.repository.js` (`lockStaffPermissionVersion`, `setStaffPermissionVersion`, details += `permission_version`), `services/permission.services.js` (`permissionSave` rewritten, `permissionSaveOnly` returns the change, `permissionHistory`), `controllers/permission.controller.js`, `validations/permission.validation.js`, `routes/permission.route.js`, `utils/check.global.permission.utils.js`, `locales/{en,zh_HK}.json` (`PERMISSIONS_STALE_VERSION`).

Tests: `node --check` on every touched file; container harness with the REAL models + repositories + service on a MariaDB copy of the tables after the SQL: list v0 → save 3 keys = v1 (3 events, 3 cache keys, 1 bus publish) → identical re-save = 0 writes / 0 events / v1 → other actor changes one key = v2 → first actor saves at v1 → 409 with `changed_since` = that one change by name → re-save at v2 as `copy` = v3 → no-version save with no change = still v3 → history 5 rows newest first → two concurrent saves at the same version: one `ok v1`, the other 409, one log row. SQL ran twice (second run stops at the guarded ALTER as intended). Deploy: SQL on staging → restart settings API. Frontend wiring: `frontend-prompt-permissions-version-audit_2026-10-23.md`.

### 267 — Administration authorities server-side (P-5): `manage_permissions` · `manage_assignment_eligibility` · `view_permission_history` + `permissions.key_value` ENUM → VARCHAR + catalogue allowlist — settings API + admin API + processing API (23 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend `user_permissions_BACKEND_ASKS_2026-10-22.md` P-5; 22 Oct package "Users and permission administration", PX-01 / PX-02, AT-057 / AT-059 ("separate account Edit from Manage permissions and Manage assignment eligibility … a mixed save is atomic"). U-0366 (who holds the keys at launch) is still open, so every gate runs in a **transitional mode**: env `AUTHORITY_GATES` = `legacy` (default, nothing to add to any .env) keeps today's gates passing beside the new keys and logs the gap; `strict` enforces only the new authorities. Flip = set `AUTHORITY_GATES=strict` on settings, admin and processing APIs after the accounts are provisioned.

- SQL `db_queries_2026-10-15/16_permission_keys_varchar_admin_keys_2026-10-23.sql` (run once): `permissions.key_value ENUM → VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL` (no data change, both indexes stay) — no more ALTER per new key; the catalogue becomes the allowlist. No rows inserted: the three keys start OFF for everyone (package: "new sensitive capabilities start off").
- settings catalogue (`get.permission.module.list.utils.js`, Users module): + `manage_permissions`, `manage_assignment_eligibility`, `view_permission_history` (y/n; labels `MANAGE_PERMISSIONS`, `MANAGE_ASSIGNMENT_ELIGIBILITY`, `VIEW_PERMISSION_HISTORY` ×2 locales). `check.global.permission.utils.js` maps them (and `view_user`) to `users_access`.
- settings `middleware/check.permission.middleware.js` `checkAuthority(authorityKey, legacyKey)`: `admin` guard bypass → `users_access` → `authorityKey = y`, else (legacy mode) `legacyKey = y` with an info log, else **403 `{ code:"MISSING_AUTHORITY", authority }`** (`MISSING_AUTHORITY` ×2 locales). Routes: `POST /permission/save` → `checkAuthority("manage_permissions", "edit_user")`; `GET /permission/history` → `checkAuthority("view_permission_history", "view_user")`.
- settings `services/permission.services.js` `permissionSave`: the catalogue for the staff type (`getPermissionModuleList(request, 0, staff_type)`) is the allowlist — unknown keys are skipped and returned as `ignored_keys[]` (legacy) or refused **422 `{ code:"UNKNOWN_PERMISSION_KEY", keys }`** before anything is written (strict). `envs.authority_gates` added to `config/envs.config.js`.
- admin API `staff/staff.service.ts`: `ELIGIBILITY_FIELDS` (the 25 is_cs / interpreter / onboarding1 / onboarding2 / post-hire / prepared_groups / ready_groups columns); `eligibilityChanges(before, staffData)` = the ones that actually change (CSV compared as sorted sets; on creation only eligibility switched ON counts); `hasEligibilityAuthority` = admin / sub-admin guard or `manage_assignment_eligibility = y`; add-staff (inside the transaction → nothing persists) and update-staff (before the write) refuse the **whole request** with 403 `{ code:"MISSING_AUTHORITY", authority:"manage_assignment_eligibility", fields }` in strict mode, log in legacy. Every eligibility change is audited into `permission_change_logs` with `source = 'eligibility'` (repository `addEligibilityChangeLogs`; `permission_version` not bumped — a different form must not make open permission editors stale). `permission.service.ts` BOOLEAN_KEYS += the three keys. lang ×4 `MISSING_AUTHORITY_ELIGIBILITY`.
- processing API `processing.prep.ready.owners.services.ts`: `GET /prep-ready-owners/defaults` → admin / sub-admin or `manage_assignment_eligibility` (legacy: change-key holders keep read access); `POST` → admin / sub-admin or `manage_assignment_eligibility` (was admin-only); `can_edit` = that authority; refusals carry `code` / `authority` (`sendBulkResult`). `envs.authority_gates` added.

Tests: settings `node --check` ×7; admin narrowed tsc (staff module + permission service / guard) 0 errors; processing narrowed tsc 0 errors in touched files. Container: `checkAuthority` 7 cases × 2 modes (admin pass; key holder pass; legacy key passes only in legacy; wrong key / nothing → 403 MISSING_AUTHORITY; employer 401); allowlist on the real service + MariaDB — legacy: 2 known keys written, 2 unknown reported in `ignored_keys`; strict: 422 with the keys, nothing written. Deploy: SQL 16 → restart settings, admin, processing. Frontend: section E of `frontend-prompt-permissions-263-264-265_2026-10-23.md`.

### 269 — Non-Owner consolidation + three-level access (P-1 / P-2): Record access levels per ownership section, Search access `off`, schema-2 migration — settings · admin · contract · processing · hrms · insurance APIs (23 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend `user_permissions_BACKEND_ASKS_2026-10-22.md` P-1 / P-2; 22 Oct package migrations "Non-Owner consolidation", "Levels and counts", AT-005 / AT-054 / AT-055. Built everything the package states; the one open question — what `restricted` means on an **owner** section (U-0368) — only holds back the owner-section readers, nothing else.

- **Catalogue** (settings `get.permission.module.list.utils.js`): Record access rows (`off | restricted | full_access`) at the top of each ownership section — `worker_record_access_interpreter_owner`, `worker_record_access_onboarding_owner`, `worker_record_access_non_owner`, `partner_record_access`, `jo_record_access_cs_owner`, `jo_record_access_non_owner`, `contract_record_access_cs_owner`, `contract_record_access_non_owner`. `*_access_type` (Search access) gains `off`. The three enable keys stay in the catalogue as `deprecated: true, replaced_by: <level key>` until the frontend has switched (then one line removes them); their `selected_value` is **derived** from the level when one is stored (`off` → `n`, else `y`). Worker owner levels never read below `restricted` (absent → `restricted`, AT-005). One `resolveSelectedValue` replaces the 27 inline `getPermission` calls. Labels `NO_ACCESS`, `RECORD_ACCESS` ×2 locales.
- **Save rules** (settings `permission.services.js`): level keys and `*_access_type` accept only `off | restricted | full_access` (422 `INVALID_PERMISSION_VALUE`, keys listed); `worker_record_access_interpreter_owner` / `_onboarding_owner` = `off` → 422 `OWNER_FLOOR`; on an account with `permission_schema_version ≥ 2` the three enable keys are dropped + reported in `ignored_keys` (legacy) or refused 422 `DEPRECATED_PERMISSION_KEY` (strict); on schema 1 they still save (the frontend's bridge keeps working). `lockStaffPermissionVersion` now returns `{ version, schema }`; `staffs.model.js` += `permission_schema_version`.
- **Readers — Non-Owner**: every reader keeps asking for the old enable key; the permission lookups answer it from the level when one is stored (`off` → `n`, otherwise `y`) and fall back to the legacy row otherwise, so deploy and migration can happen in any order and the day-one behaviour is identical (`off` = today's `n`, `restricted` / `full_access` = today's `y`; the 10-row cap stays module-wide via `*_access_type` until U-0368). admin `auth/permission.service.ts` (`withLegacyEnable` around `getPermissionByStaffId` / `getPermissionByUserId`), contract / processing / hrms `repository/permission.repository.ts` (`getPermissionByUser` → `getStoredPermissionByUser`).
- **Readers — Search access `off`** ("disables search without hiding otherwise accessible records"): a search with `*_access_type = off` is refused with `SEARCH_ACCESS_OFF` (listing without a search term unchanged) — admin `worker-list.service.ts`, `jobs.service.ts`, `employer.service.ts` (code `SEARCH_ACCESS_OFF`, lang ×4), contract `contract.dashboard.services.ts` (403), insurance `insurance.services.ts` (list). Invoice lists borrow `contract_processing_access_type` today (PM-D08 family) — untouched here.
- **SQL `17_non_owner_consolidation_2026-10-23.sql`**: `staffs.permission_schema_version TINYINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1`; **PART A** = read-only plan (per internal staff × module: enable value, access_type, resulting level; counts; the INVALID rows); **PART B** (one transaction, run once after review): insert the Non-Owner level rows (`absent / '' / n → off`, `y → *_access_type (restricted | full_access), absent → restricted`; an already stored level is kept), one `permission_change_logs` row per module (`source = schema_migration`, old = "enable=… | access_type=…", new = level) + one per deleted enable row, delete the three enable keys, set schema 2. Staff with an invalid legacy value stay on schema 1 and are listed (rejected, not guessed). Partner accounts untouched (`partner_record_access` has no legacy source — set in the editor). Re-runnable: a second run finds nothing to do.

Not in this entry (U-0368): the per-section Restricted cap / owner-level readers — the owner keys are stored and served (floor applied) but no list is narrowed by them yet.

Tests: settings `node --check` ×6; admin narrowed tsc (permission service + 3 list services) 0; contract / processing / hrms / insurance narrowed tsc 0 errors in touched files. Container: catalogue render for a migrated and a legacy staff (derived enable values `y`/`n`, owner floor `restricted`, value lists, partner row, all labels); save rules on the real service + MariaDB (422 invalid value ×2, 422 owner floor, good save, deprecated key dropped on schema 2 / saved on schema 1, strict → 422); processing repository virtual mapping (level → `y`/`n`, fallback to legacy row, null, ordinary key untouched); SQL 17 on a seeded copy — plan counts, levels (`y`+full → full_access, `y`+none → restricted, `n`/absent → off, stored level kept, invalid skipped), audit rows, enable rows deleted, schema flags, second run a no-op. Deploy: SQL 17 PART A → review → PART B → restart settings, admin, contract, processing, hrms, insurance. Frontend: section G of `frontend-prompt-permissions-263-264-265_2026-10-23.md`.

### 274 — Redis waits bounded in the admin API: `update-staff` no longer hangs when Redis is down, half-open or stalled — admin API `core/cache` (23 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend (23 Oct) — `POST update-staff` on staging hung > 30 s (client cancelled) while other endpoints answered. `updateStaff` ends with `await this.bus.publish('auth.user_changed', …)` (and `addStaff` / partner-worker / translations publish too); both ioredis clients (`InvalidationBusService`, `CacheService`) were built with `retryStrategy: () => null` and ioredis defaults — no `commandTimeout`, offline queue on. A Redis that accepts the TCP connection but never answers (half-open socket after a Redis restart / security-group change, or a frozen server) therefore parked the awaiting request for the TCP retransmit timeout (minutes); a Redis that is reachable only intermittently parked it for the connect timeout (10 s). Only the endpoints that publish were affected, which matches the report. Root cause on staging itself still needs the checks in the reply note (Redis env / `redis-cli ping` / pm2 log during a hang).

- `invalidation-bus.service.ts`: client options `connectTimeout: 2000`, `commandTimeout: 1500`, `enableOfflineQueue: false`, `retryStrategy: times => min(times*500, 5000)` (background reconnect, so the bus recovers without a restart); `publish()` runs under one 1.5 s deadline (`PUBLISH_TIMEOUT_MS`) covering `awaitReady()` (first publish after start / after a reconnect waits for `ready` instead of being queued) and the PUBLISH round-trip; timeout / not-ready / ended are swallowed and logged at debug with the reason. The injected-stub path used by `storage.spec.ts` (no `status`) is unchanged.
- `cache.service.ts`: same client options; `ready` → `redisHealthy = true`, `close` → `redisHealthy = false`, `error` handled — the in-memory fallback takes over while Redis is down (before, `redisHealthy` stayed true after a drop and every hit waited on the dead socket) and Redis is used again as soon as the reconnect succeeds.
- No contract change, no env change; `IS_REDIS_ENABLED` / `INVALIDATION_BUS_ENABLED` behave as before.

Tests: narrowed tsc — 0 errors in both files. Container, real `redis-server` 7 + the two services: live publish delivered to a subscriber (4 ms, first call after start); `SIGSTOP` on redis (connected but silent) → `publish` returns in 1503 ms, `cache.get`/`put` in ~1500 ms and serve from memory; `SIGCONT` → both clients back to `ready` by themselves, publish 1 ms; `SIGKILL` → publish 0 ms, cache from memory 0 ms; redis restarted → `ready` again within the back-off, publish delivered. Fake "accepts but never answers" server and refused port: every call ≤ 1.5 s, master switch off → no client created.

### 275 — HRMS `GET /hrms/leave-apply/type-list`: optional `?user_id=` target (Apply Leave on behalf), no exception without a policy — hrms API (23 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend (23 Oct) — on staging the admin user gets `data: { data: [] }`; Manual Update Leaves already moved to `get-leave-balance/:id`, Apply Leave still relies on `type-list`.

Why it is empty: `type-list` has always been **self-scoped** — it lists the leave types of the *caller's own* leave policy (`staffs.leave_policy_id` of the logged-in user → `leave_policy_details`). The staging admin account has no leave policy (or its staffs row has `leave_policy_id` NULL), so its own list is empty — correct for "my leave types", useless for "apply leave for staff X". `get-leave-balance/:id` is per target user, which is why it works. The result is also cached in Redis per caller (`leavetype:list:user:<id>`, `REDIS_EXPIRES_IN`, default 1800 s), so a policy fixed on the admin shows up only after the TTL / a cache clear.

- `services/leave.apply.services.ts` `leaveTypeList`: `reqParams.user_id` (optional) selects the target user; absent → caller (unchanged). Scope copied from `getLeaveBalance`: admin → anyone; others → self or a direct reportee (`getAllReportees`), otherwise `{ error: "ACCESS_DENIED" }`. Target without a staffs row / without `leave_policy_id` → `{ data: [], leave_policy_id: null, user_id }` (before: `Cannot read properties of null` → `{ error }` spread into a 200). Response gains `leave_policy_id` + `user_id`; cache key is the target's.
- `controllers/leave.apply.controller.ts` `leaveTypeList`: service `error` → 403 (`ACCESS_DENIED`, translated) or 400 instead of being spread into the 200 envelope.
- No SQL, no env. Restart hrms.

Tests: narrowed tsc — 0 errors in the two files (pre-existing model/util errors elsewhere unchanged). Frontend: Apply Leave → `GET /hrms/leave-apply/type-list?user_id=<target user_id>` (same id as `get-leave-balance/:id`); `leave_policy_id: null` → show "No leave policy assigned to this user" and disable the type select.

### 276 — `GET /permission/list?staff_id` answered "This is an Admin Account" for a normal user on staging: repository error object read as a staff row — settings API (23 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend (23 Oct) — `permission/list?staff_id=3` → 200 `{ error: "This is an Admin Account" }` for a non-admin staff.

Cause: `staffsRepository.getStaffDetailsByStaffId` returns `{ error }` (truthy) when its query fails; the three permission services only tested `!staffDetails`, so the error object went on to `staffDetails.role_guard_name` → undefined → `THIS_IS_AN_ADMIN_ACCOUNT`. The query fails on staging because 263 selects `staffs.permission_version` and SQL 14 (`14_permission_version_audit_2026-10-23.sql`) has not been applied there yet — the settings API was restarted ahead of the SQL. Same repository serves CS / onboarding / interpreter assignment in employer / worker / branch services, so those fail on staging for the same reason until SQL 14 runs.

- `permission.services.js` `permissionList`, `permissionSave`, `permissionHistory`: `staffDetails?.error` → `{ error, statusCode: 500, code: "STAFF_LOOKUP_FAILED" }` (list spreads it as `{ error, code }`, save → 500) so a DB error reads as a DB error.
- Fix for staging is data, not code: run SQL 14 → 15 → 16 → 17 (PART A then PART B), then the list works unchanged.

Tests: `node --check`. No SQL beyond the pending ones, no env.

### 283 — `change-job-owner`: owner / CS Assistant collision resolved explicitly in the same operation (`cs_assistant_action`) — admin API (26 Oct 2026)

Source: frontend (26 Oct) — the Change CS Owner dialog needs to clear the Job Offer's CS Assistant when that person becomes the owner; the API refused the pair with 422 and had no way to resolve it. CMATRIX "owner=assistant collision requires explicit assistant removal/replacement in the same reviewed operation", prototype B27-15; same keys as the Contract register (processing API `change-processing-cs-owner`).

- `jobs.controller.ts` rules: `cs_assistant_action: nullable|in:remove,replace`, `replacement_cs_assistant_staff_id: nullable|integer|exists:staffs,id`; refusal bodies carry `code`.
- `jobs.service.ts` `changeJobOwner`: when the new owner is the JO's current CS Assistant — no action → **422** `{ code: "ASSISTANT_COLLISION", cs_assistant_staff_id }` (message `JO_OWNER_IS_ASSISTANT`, unchanged); `remove` → `cs_assistant_staff_id = NULL` written in the same update; `replace` → the replacement must be an active internal staff member ≠ new owner (`422 ASSISTANT_REPLACEMENT_INVALID`, lang `JO_ASSISTANT_REPLACEMENT_INVALID` ×4) and is written in the same update. The `change_job_owner` log row's `json_data` gains `cs_assistant_resolution { action, previous_cs_assistant_id, cs_assistant_id, cs_assistant_name }`; the success `data` gains `cs_assistant_id` + `cs_assistant_action`. No collision → behaviour unchanged.

Tests: narrowed tsc 0 errors; lang JSON ×4 parse. Restart admin. Frontend: send `cs_assistant_action: "remove"` (or `"replace"` + `replacement_cs_assistant_staff_id`) with the confirmed collision; on `ASSISTANT_COLLISION` show the "Also set CS Assistant to Unassigned" confirmation and resend.

### 285 — HRMS: `staff_type` guard refused every non-admin (Leave Ledger / Manual Leave / Time Credit staff lists, leave history, manual update, time request) — HRMS API (26 Oct 2026)

Source: staging, Shubham Tester (sub-admin) → Settings → View Leave Ledger → `GET /hrms/calendar/users-list?module=view_leave_ledger` **401 "Access denied."** → page "You are not authorized to access this page", although the account holds `view_leave_ledger`. Not a permission problem: `request.userDetails` (verify.token) carries `user_id / role_id / role_guard_name / staff_id` only — **no `staff_type`** — so every `userDetails?.staff_type != "internal"` guard was `undefined != "internal"` → denied for *all* non-admin callers before the permission keys were even read. (A11 / H-P2 had already noted and bypassed this for the calendar module only.)

- `utils/task.access.utils.ts`: new `isInternalStaff(userDetails)` — admin / sub-admin pass; otherwise the staff row's `staff_type === "internal"` (unknown actor fails closed).
- Guards replaced (5): `calendar.services.ts` `usersList` (modules `manual_update_leave`, `time_entry`, `view_leave_ledger`); `leave.apply.controller.ts` `leaveHistoryList`, `manualUpdate`, `leaveEligibilityCheck`; `time.request.controller.ts` time request. Refusal is now **403** (spec G5: permission errors are 403), same as A11.
- `usersList` module `view_leave_ledger`: the non-reportees key was `manual_update_leave_non_reportees` (wrong module) → `view_leave_ledger_non_reportees` (catalogue key, same as `leaveHistoryList`).
- `usersList`: `manager_id: { [Op.notIn]: user_id }` (scalar — invalid for NOT IN) → `{ [Op.or]: { [Op.ne]: user_id, [Op.is]: null } }` for "non-reportees only" (×4 modules).

Tests: narrowed tsc — 0 errors in the four files (pre-existing model / aws-sdk / `Object.hasOn` errors elsewhere unchanged). Restart hrms. Frontend: nothing — the Leave Ledger / Manual Update Leave / Time Entry staff selectors start returning data for non-admin staff with the keys; a 403 now means the key is really missing.

### 286 — Worker Photo: Approved on / by, Uploaded on / by, File and the crop coordinates on `get-worker-detail?type=image`; Adjust Crop accepts `crop { cx, cy, r }` — admin API (27 Oct 2026)

Source: staging worker 688 → Photo: tag **Verified** but the meta rows read *Approved on —, Approved by —, Uploaded by —, Headshot · crop not set*, while the section history below says *Verified by Maidmatch Admin · 23 Oct 2026 14:59*. Same fact, two surfaces: the history is the section-status log; the meta rows are the current record (prototype `Worker_Details` `photoMetaRow` — Approved on / Approved by shown on the published pair, Uploaded on / Uploaded by / File on every record; WORKER.md §16.5.4 "submission date and source"). They were "—" because the image rows never carried those fields (250 returned only the three raw coordinate columns), and the new UI reads `crop`, `uploaded_by_name`, `approved_at`, `approved_by_name`, `file_name` and sends the crop as `crop: { cx, cy, r }` — none of which the API knew.

- `worker-detail.service.ts` type `image`: the worker select adds `image_approved_or_rejected_by / _at` (written by `worker-validate-details`); each of `default_worker_image` / `last_uploaded_worker_image` gains `crop { cx, cy, r }` (from `crop_center_x / _y / crop_radius`, null until stored), `file_name` (basename of the original key), `uploaded_at` (= `created_at`), `uploaded_by` (`created_by`), `uploaded_by_name`, `uploaded_by_role` (`admin` when `is_uploaded_by_admin`, else the creator's role — Worker / Staff / Admin); the published (default) row and the item carry `approved_at / approved_by / approved_by_name` when `image_status = verified` (else null) — the same actor / time as the "Verified by …" history line. `userImageRow` selects `is_uploaded_by_admin, created_by` too.
- `worker-write.service.ts` `cropCoordinates`: the three flat keys as before, else `crop: { cx, cy, r }` → the same three columns. `worker.controller.ts` `update-profile-image` rules `crop.cx / crop.cy` 0–100, `crop.r` 1–50. Units: the coordinates are what the client sends — the new UI (and the prototype) use **percent of the original**; 250's "pixels" wording is superseded, the columns are plain integers.

Tests: narrowed tsc 0 errors (3 files). Restart admin. Frontend: nothing to change for the meta rows (the read keys now exist) and the crop round-trips as sent; `last_uploaded_worker_image.approved_*` are always null (a pending submission is never approved).

Not changed (awaiting Priyanka's go): the regex still accepts one letter + optional `X` + 6 digits + check char and does not verify the check digit; the new UI's own `validateHKID` (`utils/utils.js`) uses the wrong weights for the 8-character form (letter ×9, digits ×8…×3 — the standard is space 36×9, letter ×8, digits ×7…×2), so a real `A123456(3)` is refused and `A123456(A)` accepted — frontend work order to follow with the backend check-digit change if wanted.

### 289 — `find-worker` (Suggest Workers): error 1267 "Illegal mix of collations … for operation 'find_in_set'" on the worker day-off test — admin API (27 Oct 2026)

Source: staging JO-2026-000329 → Suggest Workers → `POST /find-worker` `{ job_id, type:1, start_date, language:[1,3,7], share_room:"0", day_off:[1] }` → `status:false`, Prisma raw query failed, code 1267: `Illegal mix of collations (utf8mb4_general_ci,COERCIBLE) and (utf8mb4_bin,NONE) for operation 'find_in_set'` → 0 workers. The day-off expression was `FIND_IN_SET(x, COALESCE(NULLIF(workers.day_off_ids, ''), CAST(workers.day_off AS CHAR)))` (W-24): on MySQL 8 the COALESCE aggregates the column's collation with the CAST's (utf8mb4_bin, derivation NONE), and FIND_IN_SET then refuses the literal. Used by the match score (day-off weight, Flexible), the `day_off` filter, and therefore every Suggest / find-worker call that carries a day-off.

- `worker/worker-match.service.ts`: `WORKER_DAY_OFF_CSV` → `workerDayOffHas(needle)` = `IF(COALESCE(workers.day_off_ids,'') <> '', FIND_IN_SET(needle, workers.day_off_ids), FIND_IN_SET(needle, workers.day_off))` — each column tested on its own (a literal against one column / one integer never aggregates collations); the `day_off` filter binds each value twice (one placeholder per column test). Same semantics as before (CSV when present, else the legacy single day).

Tests: narrowed tsc 0 errors; expression checked on a scratch table (`day_off_ids` utf8mb4_bin, mixed NULL / '' / CSV rows → expected positions). Restart admin. Frontend: nothing.

Tests: narrowed tsc 0 errors. Restart admin. Frontend: nothing — it already reads `type_display`.

### 291 — Invoice list `total_count` ignored every filter (employer / worker tab showed "1–10 of 36" over 6 rows) — contract API (27 Oct 2026)

Source: staging Employer E000274 → Invoice tab: 6 rows, footer "1–10 of 36 invoices", 4 pages; Employer 258: 0 rows, still "36". `POST /v1/contract/invoices/list { employer_id, page, limit }` answered `total_count: 36, total_pages: 4` whatever the filter. Both list services (`services/invoices.services.ts` and `services/v1/invoices.services.ts`) call `contractInvoiceRepository.getCountAllList(await convertToObject(attrs, conditions, includes))` — one options object — while the repository signature is `(attribute, conditions, includes)`, so `where` and `include` arrived as the defaults and the count ran over the whole table. Every filtered list (employer / worker / contract tab, search, status chips, date range) paged against the table-wide total; the Restricted-access cap (`total_invoice_count < 10 ? … : 10`) also read the wrong number.

- `repository/contract.invoice.repository.ts` `getCountAllList`: accepts both call shapes (a `convertToObject` options object, or the three positional arguments); counts `distinct id` so a joined row can never inflate the total. Services untouched.

Tests: narrowed tsc — no errors in the file (pre-existing model / http-errors errors unchanged). Restart contract. Frontend: nothing — `total_count` / `total_pages` now match the rows.

### 402 — frontend · a failed fire-and-forget fetch no longer crashes the whole page (backend 292 FYI: worker 688 → Photo, 400 from worker-activity-log-list)
- File: `utils/dispatchHandler.js` (`dispatchWithHandling` returns the unwrapped promise and attaches its own catch — the rejection still reaches callers that `await` / `.catch()`; fire-and-forget callers get a non-production `console.warn` instead of an unhandled rejection); test `src/__test__/utils/dispatchHandler.test.js` (fails on the old helper, passes on the new).
- REQ-ID: Priyanka's CHANGE_HISTORY 292 ("a 400 from the activity list must not take the whole page down — render the Photo tab without activity instead").
- Cause: panel-wide since FE-5 (18 Jun). The helper `await`ed `unwrap()` and rethrew; 147 of its 157 call sites across 34 files (Worker, Employer, Settings/HRMS, Reports, Conversation, Invoice forms, components) dispatch without awaiting, so any rejected thunk — a 400, a 422, a network blip — became a `window` unhandled rejection, which the top-level `ErrorBoundary` (`captureGlobal`) turns into the "Something went wrong" fallback for the entire panel. Not specific to Photo or to the 287 CSV.
- Change: the tab now renders without the failed list (the reducer keeps the previous value), nothing else differs. The 10 awaiting callers (HRMS withdraw / approve / delete, worker check-out, status update) keep their try / catch behaviour. No call site edited.
- Status: Done — frontend.

### 332 — Job Offer CS transfer: employer CS-change request approved on `change-job-owner` (Approve CS Request + Change Owner), destination validated at commit (U-0036, OWNERS.md) — admin API `jobs` (28 Oct 2026)

Source: Priyanka 28 Oct (Employer Details "Change CS Owner" dialog — "design sahi hai?"); sheet U-0036 H/M: "remove Change CS Owner from Employer Details and the CS field from New Employer … the employer submits a reasoned CS-change request, and approval needs the new jo.approve_cs_request plus jo.change_owner, current scope and an active Manual-eligible recipient. Commit request state and transfer together or neither, and do not default-route to SuperAdmin."
- `jobs.controller.ts` `change-job-owner`: when the Job Offer carries the employer's pending request (`jobs.cs_change_reason` set — `jobCoreRow` now selects it) the transfer is the approval: on top of `jo_cs_owner_change_owner` / `jo_cs_non_owner_change_owner` the caller's context must hold `jo_cs_owner_approve_cs_request` / `jo_cs_non_owner_approve_cs_request` (catalogue keys exist since 268) — else 403 `CS_REQUEST_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` / `JO_CS_REQUEST_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`. The service already commits the owner update and the request clearance in one transaction and records the resolved request in the job log (202); no SuperAdmin fallback anywhere.
- `jobs.service.ts` `changeJobOwner`: destination validated at commit, not only in the picker — `cs_assign_type` in {manual, both} (422 `JO_OWNER_STAFF_NOT_MANUAL`), `cs_branches` contains the employer's branch (422 `JO_OWNER_STAFF_BRANCH`), `cs_employer_language` contains the employer's language id when one is stored (422 `JO_OWNER_STAFF_LANGUAGE`); `staffEligibilityRow` selects the three columns. `get-eligible-cs-staff` lists Manual-eligible CS only.
- Lang ×4: the three 422 texts + `JO_CS_REQUEST_APPROVAL_REQUIRED` (English placeholders).
Tests: narrowed tsc 0 errors (controller, service, repository). No SQL. Restart admin. Frontend: section 11 of `frontend-prompt-28oct-asks_v3_2026-10-28.md`.

### 334 — `update-employer-basic` accepts per-card saves (Employer Details · Other Contact): identity rules only when the identity card is saved; permission follows the card — admin API `employer.controller.ts` (28 Oct 2026)

Source: Priyanka 28 Oct — Employer Details tab: the two cards have separate Save buttons but one endpoint; the frontend posts the WHOLE form from either button, so a validation error on card 1 blocks card 2's Save, and a successful card-2 Save reloads the record and resets card 1's unsaved edits. The service (`updateEmployerBasicDetails`) was always partial-friendly (isset-loop; absent address / worker keys are no-ops) — only the validation forced a full payload (`title` / `surname` / `name` / `nationality` / `residential_address_line_1` required). Now: identity rules apply only when any of `title` / `surname` / `name` is present; an Other-Contact-only request (`edit_type: "other_contact"`, or just the three telephone keys) is validated on its own fields and leaves everything else untouched (`""` clears a telephone, as on the worker's W-40 path). Permission: contact-only → `employer_contact_details`, otherwise `employer_basic_details` (presence check, exactly what the former `@RequirePermission` did). Full-form posts keep working unchanged. Tests: narrowed tsc 0 errors. No SQL. Restart admin. Frontend: section 13 of `frontend-prompt-28oct-asks_v3_2026-10-28.md`.

- `Employer/newui/useEmployerDashboard.js` `setFilter("location")` resets `district: []` as well as `branch_id` (a Hong Kong district stayed applied, invisibly, after switching Location to Macau; the server applies exactly what is sent, backend 321).

### 414 — frontend · Employer Details · each card posts only its own fields (backend 334, ask 13)
- `Employer/newui/details/tabs/useEmployerBasicForm.js`: `saveCard(card)` validates and posts ONLY that card's fields + `employer_id` — Other Contact adds `edit_type: "other_contact"` (`EMPLOYER_EDIT_TYPE`); the identity card posts everything else (`EMPLOYER_CARD_META_FIELDS` = `employer_id` / `spoken_language` belong to neither). The record re-read after a save (or an address / current-worker change) keeps the other card's unsaved edits by value (a card's inputs unmount when the other card is edited, so RHF's dirty registry is not relied on); the saved card's fields take the server's values. `cancelCard(card)` restores only that card. The server's refusal is returned as `cardErrors[card]` and rendered on that card (`EmployerBasicCard` / `OtherContactCard` `error` prop, role=alert), never page-level.
- `EmployerDetailsTab.js`: Save / Cancel per card through the two calls; `OTHER_CONTACT_FIELDS` stays the Other Contact field list.
- Test: `src/__test__/employer/asks28Oct.test.js` (411–414: 10 tests).
- Status: Done — frontend.

### 429 — frontend · shared · HKID check digit: the single-letter prefix is space-padded (U-0399, Priyanka 29 Oct)
- `utils/utils.js` `validateHKID`: the 8-character form used the wrong weights (letter ×9, digits ×8..×3), so a real `A123456(3)` was refused and `A123456(A)` accepted. Now the standard algorithm (prototype `v28HkidValid`): eight positions weighted 9..2 with a leading space worth 36 before a single-letter prefix; the two-letter form is unchanged. Shape rules unchanged until U-0399 is ruled (second letter X only, as the server's regex). Every HKID reader (worker Basic / Edit Worker, employer Basic / New Employer, invoice quick-add) shares this helper.

It now updates the reportee row (`repoteesLeaves[id].status`) and never touches `leaves`; the bulk reducer no longer writes bare ids into `leaves` (both hooks refresh after success).

### 439 — frontend · HRMS · My Profile: per-card save, validation, messages, DOB format (handoff §B.2–B.5, backend 339–341)
- `hrms/profile/ProfileBasicInfoTab.js`: each card posts ONLY its keys (`PROFILE_CARD_FIELDS` in `settingsNewUiConstants.js`: Official = mobile_number · birth_date · address1; Emergency; Personal; Bank; Documents = the three links) after validating those fields; `email` (read-only) is never sent. The profile re-read after a save keeps the other cards' unsaved edits by value (a card's inputs unmount in read mode, so RHF's dirty registry is not relied on); Cancel restores only that card. A 400 `message` lands on the card that saved (`ProfileInfoSection` `error` prop, role=alert); a 403 "Company mobile already exists" lands under Mobile. Date of Birth reads in the Joined Company format (`PROFILE_JOINED_DATE_FORMAT`).

### 444 — frontend · Settings · Leave Policy: Carry Forward Max / Encash Max dropped on save (handoff 29 Oct v8 §I, backend 358)
- The flag meant the opposite on each side: the server reads `carry_forward_limit` / `encashment_limit` `"y"` as "a max applies" and `"n"` as no limit; the client sent `LIMIT_FLAG.NO_LIMIT` (`"y"`) for No Limit and `"n"` for a max, so every entered max was dropped and re-opened empty (none of No Limit / Percentage / Value selected, row "—").
- `settingsNewUiConstants.js`: `CAP_FLAG = { HAS_MAX: "y", NO_LIMIT: "n" }` for the two cap flags only; `LIMIT_FLAG` is unchanged and still drives `expiry_value_limit` (correct on both sides).
- `leave-policy/leavePolicyConfig.js`: `toRowPayload` sends `CAP_FLAG` for carry-forward and encashment and an empty `*_max_type` with No Limit; `fromApiRow` ticks No Limit on `"n"`; `capMaxLabel` (new) reads "No limit" on the flag, else the max with `%` for a percentage; `leavePolicyRowColumns.js` uses it for both max columns (a No Limit row used to read "0").
- Existing data: backend SQL 22 turns the impossible `"y"`-without-max rows into `"n"`; rows stored `"n"` before this fix are either a real No Limit or a dropped max and cannot be told apart — HR re-opens those policies and re-enters the max where there was one.
- Test: `src/__test__/settings/hrmsHandoff29Oct.test.js` §I.
- Status: Done — frontend (no deploy dependency).

### 445 — frontend · Settings · Leave Policy: "Add to Policy" refused with no message (handoff 29 Oct v9 §I.2, backend 359)

- `LeaveTypeForm.js`: the max field shows the type error as well (`maxWithLimit` rendered only the max error, so the form refused silently); max and threshold inputs step by 1 (`WHOLE_STEP`).
- `useLeavePolicyForm.js` / `LeavePolicyFormPage.js`: Save is disabled while the row editor is open, with "Please add item first" beside it, so edits in Enter Details are never dropped silently. A 400 on the rows save (backend 359) already reaches the toast through `showSuccessOrError` (verified).
- New en key (English only, veto pending): `key_encash_type_is_required`. Threshold went whole-number with the two max fields (Priyanka's "max / threshold columns are integers"); say if decimals are wanted there.
- Test: `src/__test__/settings/hrmsHandoff29Oct.test.js` §I.2.
- Status: Done — frontend.

### 450 — frontend · Contract record · stage colours follow the HTML prototype (Shubham, 29 Oct 17:40)
- Ruling: the status colour rules on the Contract record (stage rail chips, panel tags, Change Status menu dots, sub-tab dots, dashboard stage cells) follow the prototype's `TONE` / `toneName()` (Contract_Details app.js), not PROCESS.md §3.5.1. Our build had mixed the two: Authorization Ready was purple with the MD hex (`#E0CDF0`), the menu dots were lighter shades, gray used our own greys.
- `contractConstants.js`: `CD_TOKEN_BLUE` = Ready to Submit + Authorization Ready; `CD_TOKEN_PURPLE` = [] (the prototype set is deliberately empty). `StageTitle.js` `stageToken`: Not Ready reads the `red` tone (the `muted` class stays as an alias in CSS).
- CSS (`stage-panels.css`, `ContractDetails.css`, `ContractsDashboard.css`): purple and gray tag / tok / pill values = prototype TONE; every status dot (menu, `g1-subtab`, `mcdt-subtabs`) = the tone's text colour `TONE.*.c` (red `oklch(.5 .1 28)`, green `.49 .13 150`, blue `.5 .13 230`, purple `.5 .16 305`, teal `.45 .1 195`, orange `.47 .17 58`, gray `#5a626e`); the Not Ready menu dot had no rule at all and fell to the default grey.
- Unchanged on purpose: the stage-rail icon is chosen by tone (`PILL_ICON`), the prototype picks it by status name (`STATUS_ICON`) — an icon rule, not a colour rule; open point.
- Test: `src/__test__/processing/stageTones.test.js`.
- Status: Done — frontend.

The old regex guessed amber / green from the status slug (Ready to submit read orange, Consulate Submitted read green). The visa / deployment / medical sub-stage documents store the status as a slug (`letter_of_authorization_ready`, `visa_released`, …) while the options carry numeric ids, so on those panels no option was ever "current": nothing was ticked and picking the current status sent the request, which the server refused with "Process status must be different from the current status."
- `contractConstants.js` `isCurrentStatusOption`: matches by id, by name, by the slug of the name, or by the slug's token label. `StagePanel.js` `StatusMenu`: the current option is `aria-checked`, bold with the prototype's blue tick (`g1-menu-check`), and its click is inert (prototype `setStatus` returns early on the same label; Mubin asked for disabled / greyed — the HTML shows it checked instead, so that is what we follow).

No. Released, Released); `CD_COMPACT` shortens Auth Ready on the cards (prototype `COMPACT_STATUS`, full text stays in the card title). `contractDashboardUtils.js` `toneFor` / `isTerminalToken` / `compactToken`, `StageTitle.js` `stageToken` and `stageCardIcon` normalise first. The sub-stage status option keeps its API name "Letter of Authorization Ready".

- `WkdSendTemplateModal.js` (worker · employer · contract Send Template): Open WhatsApp used the prototype's `window.open('whatsapp://', '_blank')`. Chrome opens a new tab first and then asks the OS to handle the scheme, so a blank `about:blank` tab stays open; without WhatsApp Desktop installed that blank tab is the whole result. The scheme is now launched in the current tab (`utils.launchWhatsAppApp`, `WHATSAPP_APP_SCHEME` in `workerModuleConstants.js`): the "Open WhatsApp?" prompt appears when the app is installed, nothing else changes on screen, and the prototype's rule holds — no recipient, no message, nothing sent or logged; the Copy / notes stay as they were.

### 470 — frontend · Contract record · Contract stage: panel tag / status menu stale after a status change (Mubin, 30 Oct)
- `processing-detail/ProcessingDetail.js` + `contract-tab/ProcessingContractTab.js`: the Contract tab is the one panel that uses the record page's shared status save. That save refreshed the record row (rail), the status list and the activity, but the tab's own details (loaded once on mount) feed the panel tag, the "Last updated" stamp and the menu's current option. After Ready → Not Ready the rail said Not Ready while the panel still said Ready, the menu still ticked Ready, and picking Ready was inert (current option) until a page reload. The shared save now returns its promise and the tab re-reads its details after a successful save.
- Not a permission fault: Change status is gated on Stage Actions `change_status` (or admin); the Prepared / Ready owner register (`sign_off`) gates only the Prepared / Ready / CS tick marks, which is why Mubin could change the status without being an owner.

- Scope: `utils/selectScope.js` `selectScopeFor(pathname)` → `kit` | `legacy` from the route config's first segment (Invoice `invoices`, Insurance `insurance` / `insurances`, Chat `chat-view` / `altchat` / `chats` = legacy); `Layout/AppShell.jsx` writes it on `<body data-mc-select>` on every route change, so portalled dialogs (Follow Up editor, drawers, Send Template) are covered.
- No component changed: form logic, react-hook-form registration and every `onChange` stay native.
- Test: `src/__test__/components/selectKitScope.test.js`.
- Status: Done — frontend.

### 497 — frontend · i18n · fallback snapshot brought to parity with the admin locale; live regenerate guarded (gap list Worker #9 "fallback snapshot regenerate"; 3 Nov)
- Finding: `npm run i18n:snapshot` would have overwritten `src/i18n/fallback/*.json` from the staging publish, which is v1 of 14 Sep 2026 (3235 keys; zh-HK is a copy of the English for 3234 of them; 19 values are the pre-cleanup text such as "SETTINGS", "Whatsup Number", "visa_expiry_date is required"; 123 keys are ones the admin locale has since retired). That would drop the 489 keys both teams added to the snapshot by hand since September and import the 19 regressed values. The fallback backs only the OTA `useTranslation().t()` chain, which no screen calls (only `index.js` mounts the provider); `translation()` reads `public/locales` through i18next, and only a fetched live bundle — never the snapshot — is mirrored over it.
- `src/i18n/fallback/en.json`: the 109 keys the admin locale lacks are dropped (none is referenced anywhere in `src`, by whole word or by a `key_${…}` builder); the 50 values that had drifted from the admin locale now carry its text (the admin locale is the source, CH 477 rule). `zh-HK.json`: its 74 keys the admin locale lacks are dropped; values untouched. `id.json` / `zh-CN.json` are empty and unchanged.
- `src/__test__/settings/i18nOrphanBaseline.json`: the two fallback entries are now empty (the baseline may only shrink); the seed CSV and the contract mirror entries are unchanged.
- `scripts/i18n-snapshot.js`: default manifest is the app's (`i18n/staging/admin/manifest.json`, as `src/i18n/config.js`; it pointed at the retired `i18n/admin/` sheet pipeline); a publish that lacks keys the committed snapshot holds is refused with the first five named, `--force` overrides.
- Not changed: `scripts/admin-sheet-seed.csv` (retired pipeline artefact, its own baseline), the contract repo's mirror, the staging publish itself. The bucket now sends `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, so the stale v1 bundle IS fetched and mirrored over `public/locales` wherever the staging manifest is configured (`.env.production`): the 19 regressed strings are visible there today → open point "i18n · the staging translation publish is older than the code" for the pipeline owner.
- Verified: `node scripts/i18n-snapshot.js` against the live manifest now exits 1 ("en publish (v1) lacks 468 key(s) the committed snapshot holds") and writes nothing.
- Test: `src/__test__/i18n/fallbackParity.test.js` (fails on the old tree: orphans, drifted values, old default manifest).
- Status: Done — frontend.

### 498 — frontend · Deployment sub-stage heads stamp every save (handoff v25 §AA, backend 385; 3 Nov)

The new values are UTC without a zone (385), so `StagePanel.js` gains `parseStamp(value, utc)` and a `utc` prop on `StagePanelHead`; `lastUpdatedText` shows the viewer's local clock for them and keeps the plain parse for every other caller. Deployment Medical reads its own document (`last_status_updated_at || updated_at`) and needed no change.
